GCM both encrypts and authenticates, so a modified\n *       token or a wrong passphrase fails loudly instead of returning garbage.</li>\n *   <li>The key is stretched from the caller's passphrase with PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256\n *       ({@value #PBKDF2_ITERATIONS} iterations) using a fresh random salt, so no\n *       key file is required and two tokens for the same person never match.</li>\n *   <li>A fresh salt and a fresh IV are generated for every call, which is what\n *       makes repeated encryption of the same input non-repeating and safe.</li>\n * </ul>\n *\n * <p>Only the name and email are inside the encrypted blob; the salt and IV are\n * public by design and are not secret.\n */\npublic final class PiiCrypto {\n\n    /** Rejects anything this class cannot handle, with a PiiCryptoException(String message) {\n            super(message);\n        }\n\n        PiiCryptoException(String message, Throwable cause) {\n            super(message, cause);\n        }\n    }\n\n = 254;\n    static final int PASSPHRASE_MAX_CHARS = 1024;\n\n    private static final int MAX_FIELD_BYTES = 65535;\n    private static final String KEY_DERIVATION_ALGORITHM = the person's name, 1-{@value #NAME_MAX_CHARS} characters\n     * @param email       the email address, 1-{@value #EMAIL_MAX_CHARS} characters\n     * @param passphrase  the secret to derive the key from; never {@code null} or empty\n     * @return the encrypted record as one Base64 line, safe to store in a column or file\n     */\n    public static String encrypt(String name, String email, char[] passphrase) throws PiiCryptoException {\n        requireValidName(name);\n        requireValidEmail(email);\n        requireValidPassphrase(passphrase);\n\n        byte[] salt = randomBytes(SALT_LENGTH_BYTES);\n        byte[] iv = randomBytes(IV_LENGTH_BYTES);\n        SecretKey key = deriveKey(passphrase, salt);\n        try {\n            Cipher cipher = } catch (GeneralSecurityException e) {\n            throw new PiiCryptoException(\"encryption failed: \" + describe(e), e);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Decrypts a token produced by {@link #encrypt} and returns the original values.\n     *\n     * @throws PiiCryptoException if the token is malformed, was produced by a different\n     *         version of this code, or if the passphrase is wrong or the token was altered\n     */\n    public static PiiRecord decrypt(String token, char[] passphrase) throws PiiCryptoException {\n        requireValidPassphrase(passphrase);\n        if (token == null) {\n            throw new PiiCryptoException(\"the encrypted text is missing\");\n        }\n\n        byte[] container = decodeToken(token);\n        if (container.length < MIN_CONTAINER_BYTES) {\n            throw new PiiCryptoException(\"the encrypted text is too short to be one of our records\");\n        }\n\n        ByteBuffer buffer = ByteBuffer.wrap(container);\n try {\n            Cipher cipher = Cipher.getInstance(CIPHER_TRANSFORMATION);\n            cipher.init(Cipher.DECRYPT_MODE, key, new GCMParameterSpec(TAG_LENGTH_BITS, iv));\n            plaintext = It is not an RFC 5322 parser and it accepts\n     * non-ASCII addresses, because the encryption does not depend on the address being valid.\n     */\n    static void requireValidEmail(String email) throws PiiCryptoException {\n        if (email == null) {\n            throw new PiiCryptoException(\"the email address is missing\");\n        }\n        if (email.length() > EMAIL_MAX_CHARS) {\n            throw new PiiCryptoException(\"the email address is longer than \" + EMAIL_MAX_CHARS + \" characters\");\n        }\n        requireNoControlCharacters(email, \"email address\");\n\n        int firstAt = email.indexOf('@');\n        if (firstAt < 0) {\n            throw new PiiCryptoException(\"the email address must contain an '@'\");\n        }\n        if (email.indexOf('@', firstAt + 1) >= 0) {\n            throw new PiiCryptoException(\"the email address must contain exactly one '@'\");\n        }\n\n        String localPart = email.substring(0, firstAt);\n        String domain = email.substring(firstAt + 1);\n        if (localPart.isEmpty()) {\n            throw new PiiCryptoException(\"the email address has nothing before the '@'\");\n        }\n        if (domain.isEmpty()) {\n            throw new PiiCryptoException(\"the email address has nothing after the '@'\");\n        }\n        if (localPart.startsWith(\".\") || localPart.endsWith(\".\") || localPart.contains(\"..\")) {\n            throw new PiiCryptoException(\"the part of the email address before the '@' has a badly placed '.'\");\n        }\n        for (int i = 0; i < domain.length(); i++) {\n            char c = domain.charAt(i);\n            boolean allowed = (c >= 'a' && c <= 'z') || (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z') || (c >= '0' && c <= '9')\n                    || c == '.' || c == '-' || c > 0x7F;\n            if (!allowed) {\n                throw new PiiCryptoException(\n                        \"the email address contains a character that host names may not use: '\" + c + \"'\");\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    static void requireValidPassphrase(char[] passphrase) throws PiiCryptoException {\n        if (passphrase == null || passphrase.length == 0) {\n            throw new PiiCryptoException(\"a passphrase is required\");\n        }\n        if (passphrase.length > PASSPHRASE_MAX_CHARS) {\n            throw new PiiCryptoException(\"the passphrase is longer than \" + PASSPHRASE_MAX_CHARS + \" characters\");\n        }\n        boolean hasVisibleCharacter = false;\n        for (int i = 0; i < passphrase.length; i++) {\n            char c = passphrase[i];\n            if (c == 0) {\n                // PBEKeySpec silently stops at a NUL, so \"abc\\0\" and \"abc\" would\n                // derive the same key. Refuse rather than keep two equal passphrases.\n                throw new PiiCryptoException(\"the passphrase must not contain a NUL character\");\n            }\n            if (Character.isHighSurrogate(c)) {\n                if (i + 1 >= passphrase.length || !Character.isLowSurrogate(passphrase[i + 1])) {\n                    // An unpaired surrogate is turned into '?' by PBEKeySpec, which\n                    // would let a different passphrase derive the same key.\n                    throw new PiiCryptoException(\"the passphrase has a broken supplementary character\");\n                }\n                i++;\n            }\n            if (!Character.isWhitespace(c)) {\n                hasVisibleCharacter = true;\n            }\n        }\n        if (!hasVisibleCharacter) {\n            throw new PiiCryptoException(\"the passphrase must not be only whitespace\");\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static void requireNoControlCharacters(String value, String what) throws PiiCryptoException {\n        for (int i = 0; i < value.length(); i++) {\n            char c = value.charAt(i);\n            if (c < 0x20 || c == 0x7F) {\n                throw new PiiCryptoException(\"the \" + what + \" contains a control character (code \" + (int) c\n                        + \") that must be removed\");\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    // ------------------------------------------------------------------- payload\n\n    /**\n     * Packs both fields as {@code version | len | name | len | email}. The two byte\n     * lengths are what make the framing exact: a name that itself contains a\n     * separator such as {@code \"|\"} or an {@code @} cannot be mistaken for the email.\n     */\n    private static byte[] encodePayload(String name, String email) throws PiiCryptoException {\n        byte[] nameBytes = name.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n        byte[] emailBytes = email.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n        if (nameBytes.length > MAX_FIELD_BYTES || emailBytes.length > MAX_FIELD_BYTES) {\n            throw new PiiCryptoException(\"the record is too large to encrypt\");\n        }\n        ByteBuffer buffer = ByteBuffer.allocate(1 + 2 + nameBytes.length + 2 + emailBytes.length);\n        buffer.put(PAYLOAD_VERSION);\n        buffer.putShort((short) nameBytes.length);\n        buffer.put(nameBytes);\n        buffer.putShort((short) emailBytes.length);\n        buffer.put(emailBytes);\n        return buffer.array();\n    }\n\n    private static PiiRecord decodePayload(byte[] plaintext) throws PiiCryptoException {\n        ByteBuffer buffer = ByteBuffer.wrap(plaintext);\n        if (buffer.get() != PAYLOAD_VERSION) {\n            throw new PiiCryptoException(\"the decrypted record uses an unsupported layout\");\n        }\n        byte[] nameBytes = readLengthPrefixed(buffer, \"name\");\n        byte[] emailBytes = readLengthPrefixed(buffer, \"email address\");\n        if (buffer.hasRemaining()) {\n            throw new PiiCryptoException(\"the decrypted record has unexpected trailing bytes\");\n        }\n        return new PiiRecord(decodeUtf8(nameBytes, \"name\"), decodeUtf8(emailBytes, \"email address\"));\n    }\n\n    private static byte[] readLengthPrefixed(ByteBuffer buffer, String what) throws PiiCryptoException {\n        if (buffer.remaining() < 2) {\n            throw new PiiCryptoException(\"the decrypted record is truncated before the \" + what);\n        }\n        int length = buffer.getShort() & 0xFFFF;\n        if (length == 0 || length > buffer.remaining()) {\n            throw new PiiCryptoException(\"the decrypted record has a corrupt length for the \" + what);\n        }\n        byte[] field = new byte[length];\n        buffer.get(field);\n        return field;\n    }\n\n    private static String decodeUtf8(byte[] field, String what) throws PiiCryptoException {\n public static void main(String[] args) {\n        long startedAt = System.nanoTime();\n\n        roundTripAscii();\n        roundTripNonAscii();\n        roundTripRandomised();\n check(\"ascii name survives\", name, back.name());\n        check(\"ascii email survives\", email, back.email());\n    }\n\n    private static void roundTripNonAscii() {\n        // Built from code Record that the\n                // rejection happened, so the case cannot silently vanish from coverage.\n                check(\"adversarial pair rejected loudly: \" + pair[0].replace((char) 0x1F, // Sized fixtures must be built from real characters. new char[n] is n NULs,\n        // which trips every blank and control-character guard and proves nothing.\n        String maxName = \"n\".repeat(PiiCrypto.NAME_MAX_CHARS);\n        String maxEmail = \"u\".repeat(60) + \"@\" + \"d\".repeat(60) + \".\" + \"c\".repeat(60) + \".com\";\n        check(\"max size email fits the limit\",     check(\"max size email survives\", maxEmail, back.email());\n\n        String multibyteEmail = (\"\\u00E9\".repeat(60)) + \"@\" + (\"\\u00E9\".repeat(60)) + \".com\";\n        PiiRecord utf =         rejects(() -> PiiCrypto.encrypt(\"n\".repeat(PiiCrypto.NAME_MAX_CHARS + 1), \"a@b.co\", GOOD),\n                        \"longer than\"));\n    }\n\n    private static void encryptionIsNotRepeatable() {\n        String name = \"Ada Lovelace\";\n        String email = \"ada@example.com\";\n        String first = encrypt(name, email, GOOD);\n        String second = encrypt(name, email, GOOD);\n        check(\"two encryptions of one record differ\", true, !first.equals(second));\n        check(\"both tokens still open to the same record\", true,\n                decrypt(first, GOOD).equals(decrypt(second, GOOD)));\n        check(\"the salt is different\", true, !saltOf(first).equals(saltOf(second)));\n        check(\"the iv is different\", true, passphrase is refused\", true, message != null);\n        check(\"wrong passphrase mentions the passphrase\", true, message != null && message.contains(\"passphrase\"));\n        check(\"the wrong passphrase is not echoed back\", false, message != null && message.contains(\"horse battery staple\"));\n    }\n\n    private static void tamperingRefused() {\n        String token = encrypt(\"Ada Lovelace\", \"ada@example.com\", GOOD);\n        byte[] container = Base64.getDecoder().decode(token);\n\n        byte[] flippedCiphertext = container.clone();\n        int ciphertextStart = 1 + flipped salt byte is refused\", true, refuses(() ->\n                PiiCrypto.decrypt(encode(flippedSalt), GOOD)));\n\n        byte[] flippedIv = container.clone();\n        flippedIv[1 + new byte[ciphertextStart + 4];\n        System.arraycopy(container, 0, truncated, 0, truncated.length);\n        check(\"a truncated token is refused\", true, refuses(() ->\n                PiiCrypto.decrypt(encode(truncated), GOOD)));\n\n        byte[] wrongVersion = container.clone();\n        wrongVersion[0] = 7;\n        String message = refusalMessage(() -> PiiCrypto.decrypt(encode(wrongVersion), GOOD));\n        check(\"an unknown format version is refused\", true, message != null);\n        check(\"an unknown format version says so\", true, message != null && message.toLowerCase().contains(\"version\"));\n    }\n\n    private static void malformedTokensRefused() {\n        check(\"null token is refused\", true, refuses(() -> PiiCrypto.decrypt(null, GOOD)));\n        check(\"empty token is refused\", true,\n                refuses(() -> PiiCrypto.decrypt(\"\", GOOD), \"empty\"));\n        check(\"whitespace token is refused\", true,\n                refuses(() -> PiiCrypto.decrypt(\"   \\t \", GOOD), \"empty\"));\n        check(\"non-Base64 token is refused\", true,\n                refuses(() -> PiiCrypto.decrypt(\"not a valid token!!\", GOOD), \"Base64\"));\n        // The MIME decoder would throw the '-' and '!' away and decode this to\n        // something short, so the strict decoder's message is the thing to assert.\n        check(\"Base64url alphabet is refused\", true,\n                refuses(() -> PiiCrypto.decrypt(\"YWJj-_ZA\", GOOD), \"Base64\"));\n        check(\"too-short decoded token is refused\", true,\n                refuses(() -> PiiCrypto.decrypt(encode(new byte[10]), GOOD), \"too short\"));\n        check(\"two-line token is refused\", true,\n                refuses(() -> PiiCrypto.decrypt(\"YWJj\\nZA==\", GOOD), \"single unbroken line\"));\n        check(\"null passphrase is refused\", true, refuses(() -> PiiCrypto.decrypt(\"YWJjZA==\", null), \"passphrase\"));\n\n        // Padding is not produced by encrypt() but must still be readable, so that a\n        // token copied out of a tool that re-pads it keeps working.\n        String token = encrypt(\"Ada Lovelace\", \"ada@example.com\", GOOD);\n        String repadded = Base64.getEncoder().encodeToString(Base64.getDecoder().decode(token));\n        check(\"a re-padded token still decrypts\", \"ada@example.com\", decrypt(repadded, GOOD).email());\n        check(\"a token with surrounding blanks still decrypts\", \"Ada Lovelace\",\n                decrypt(\"  \" + token + \"  \", GOOD).name());\n    }\n\n    private static void tokenIsBase64AndLeaksNothing() {\n        String name = \"Ada Lovelace\";\n        String email = \"ada@example.com\";\n        String token = \"u!#$%&'*-/=?^_`{|}~z@example.com\", \"digits123@ex-ample.com\", \"\\u00E9@example.com\",\n                \"a@\\u00E9xample.com\",\n        };\n        for (String email : accepted) {\n private static void keyDerivationIsReproducible() {\n        byte[] container = Base64.getDecoder().decode(encrypt(\"Ada Lovelace\", \"ada@example.com\", GOOD));\n        byte[] salt = new (PiiCrypto.PiiCryptoException e) {\n            throw new AssertionError(\"encrypt(\" + name + \", \" + email + \") was refused: \" + e.getMessage(), e);\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static action.run();\n            return true;\n        } catch (Exception e) {\n            return false;\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static boolean refuses(Action action) {\n        return return null;\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static void record(String what) {\n        FAILURES.add(what);\n    }\n\n    private static void check(String what, Object expected, Object actual) {\n       check(\"the plain text really contains the email\", true, email.contains(\"ada@example.com\"));\n        check(\"the payload really contains both fields\", true, (name +        check(\"token has no padding characters\", false, token.contains(\"=\"));\n        check(\"token is valid Base64\", true, Base64.getDecoder().decode(token).length > 0);\n        check(\"token void recordToStringIsRedacted() {\n        PiiRecord record = new PiiRecord(\"Ada Lovelace\", \"ada@example.com\");\n        String printed = record.toString();\n        check(\"toString is not empty\", true, !printed.isEmpty());\n        check(\"toString hides the name\", false, printed.contains(\"Lovelace\"));\n        check(\"toString hides the email\", false, nullNameRejected = true;\n        }\n        check(\"a null name is refused by the record\", true, nullNameRejected);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * The key must come from (passphrase, salt) and nothing void keyDerivationFollowsTheToken() {\n        String token = encrypt(\"Ada Lovelace\", \"ada@example.com\", GOOD);\n        byte[] container = Base64.getDecoder().decode(token);\n        byte[] salt = Arrays.copyOfRange(container, 1, 1 + PiiCrypto.SALT_LENGTH_BYTES);\n        check(\"the token carries a full salt\", PiiCrypto.SALT_LENGTH_BYTES, salt.length);\n        check(\"a token re-opened twice gives the same answer\", true,\n                decrypt(token, GOOD).equals(decrypt(token, GOOD)));\n        check(\"a token does not open under a one-character-different passphrase\", true,\n                refuses(() -> PiiCrypto.decrypt(token, chars(\"correct horse battery staplb\"))));\n        check(\"a token does not open under a longer passphrase\", true,\n                refuses(() -> chars(\"correct horse battery stap\"))));\n    }\n\n    // --------------------------------------------------------------------- helpers\n\n    private interface Action {\n        void run() throws Exception;\n    }\n\n    private static char[] chars(String value) {\n        return value.toCharArray();\n    }\n\n    private static String repeat(char c, int times) {\n        StringBuilder builder = new StringBuilder(times);\n        for (int i = 0; i < times; i++) {\n            builder.append(c);\n        }\n        return builder.toString();\n    }\n\n    private static String randomString(Random random, String alphabet, int length) {\n        StringBuilder builder = new StringBuilder(length);\n        for (int i = 0; i < length; i++) {\n            builder.append(alphabet.charAt(random.nextInt(alphabet.length())));\n        }\n        return builder.toString();\n    }\n\n    private static String saltOf(String token) {\n        byte[] container = Base64.getDecoder().decode(token);\n        return Base64.getEncoder().encodeToString(\n                Arrays.copyOfRange(container, 1, 1 + PiiCrypto.SALT_LENGTH_BYTES));\n    }\n\n    private static String ivOf(String token) {\n        byte[] container = Base64.getDecoder().decode(token);\n        return Base64.getEncoder().encodeToString(Arrays.copyOfRange(container,\n                1 + PiiCrypto.SALT_LENGTH_BYTES, 1 + PiiCrypto.SALT_LENGTH_BYTES + PiiCrypto.IV_LENGTH_BYTES));\n    }\n\n    private static String encode(byte[] container) {\n        return Base64.getEncoder().withoutPadding().encodeToString(container);\n    }\n\n    private static String encrypt(String name, String email, char[] passphrase) {\n        try {\n            derivations++;\n            return PiiCrypto.encrypt(name, email, passphrase);\n        } catch (PiiCrypto.PiiCryptoException e) {\n            throw new AssertionError(\"encrypt(\" + shorten(name) + \", \" + email + There is no Maven or Gradle\nbuild and no third-party library to download.\n\n## The files\n\n| File | What it is |\n| --- | --- |\n| `src/PiiCrypto.java` | The encryption and decryption. This is the interesting one. |\n| `src/PiiRecord.java` | A name and an email address that print themselves redacted. |\n| `src/PiiApp.java` | The command line. Asks for the values, encrypts, decrypts, prints. |\n| `test/PiiCryptoTest.java` | 194 checks. Run it with `java PiiCryptoTest`. |\n| `.mutation/` | A harness that deliberately breaks the code, to check the tests notice. |\n| `build.sh` | Compiles everything, runs the tests, then runs the demo. |\n\n## How to run it\n\n```\njavac -d out src/*.java\njava -cp out PiiApp \"Ada Lovelace\" \"ada@example.com\"\n```\n\nThat asks for a passphrase on the terminal, without echoing what you type. On a\nmachine with no terminal (a script, a container, a cron job) put the passphrase in an\nenvironment variable instead:\n\n```\nPII_PASSPHRASE='a long phrase you will remember' java -cp out PiiApp \"Ada Lovelace\" \"ada@example.com\"\n```\n\nOr build and try everything in one go:\n\n```\n./build.sh\n```\n\nHere is what the program prints. Every run is a little different, because the\nencryption is deliberately not repeatable (see below).\n\n```\nPlain text (what you typed, this is the sensitive part)\n  name : Ada Lovelace\n  email: ada@example.com\n\nEncrypted with AES-256-GCM (store this, it is safe to store)\n  AaHIfNklXbPi5fC0eqqbKaG0K3ao3gHFklTOcrLw/kRX26/RKScrscLf4jGN/Kpc10OHXBmarqMgIoDd1awkFaaLy2xECp+KkNX9R7E\n\nSame input encrypted a second time (a different token, same record)\n  AaMbm26J1+TkAETgAALrO29lK8DkESQb6szpHR4QKC87pbXMLcq7WSwBaH7+XjetKBOMZnobfmOqli40b5M54oOHT5GFEjBnlwWoiYc\n  tokens identical: false\n\nDecrypted again with the same passphrase\n  name : Ada Lovelace\n  email: ada@example.com\n\nRound trip identical to the input: true\n\nSame token with a wrong passphrase, refused as expected\n  cannot open this record: the passphrase is wrong, or the encrypted text was altered\n```\n\nExit codes: `0` fine, `1` you need to fix something (bad email, short passphrase),\n`2` something genuinely unexpected.\n\n## Using it from your own code\n\n```java\nchar[] passphrase = \"a long phrase you will remember\".toCharArray();\n\nString token = PiiCrypto.encrypt(\"Ada Lovelace\", \"ada@example.com\", passphrase);\n// store `token`: one line of text, safe to put in a database column\n\nPiiRecord person = PiiCrypto.decrypt(token, passphrase);\nSystem.out.println(person.name());   // Ada Lovelace\nSystem.out.println(person.email());  // ada@example.com\n\nArrays.fill(passphrase, '\\0');       // wipe it from memory when done\n```\n\nAnything wrong throws `PiiCrypto.PiiCryptoException` with a message you can show a\nperson. It never returns a partly-correct answer.\n\n## What actually happens, in plain English\n\n1. **A key is made from your passphrase.** AES needs a key of an exact size, and a\n   passphrase is whatever length you happen to type. So the passphrase is first run\n   through PBKDF2 with HMAC-SHA256 for 210,000 rounds, which turns it into a proper\n   256-bit AES key and, more importantly, makes guessing it slow.\n2. **Two random values are drawn, freshly, every time.** A 16-byte *salt*, which is\n   fed into step 1 so that two records protected with the same passphrase still get\n   different keys, and a 12-byte *initial value* (IV), which AES-GCM needs and which\n   must never be reused with the same key.\n3. **AES encrypts the pair, in GCM mode.** GCM is the part that matters most: besides\n   hiding the text it also produces a short check value (a 128-bit tag) that depends\n   on the whole record *and* on the key. That is what makes tampering visible.\n4. **Everything is packed into one line.** The layout is:\n\n   ```\n   version(1 byte) | salt(16) | iv(12) | encrypted text + tag\n   ```\n\n   and the whole thing is Base64 encoded so it can live in a text column, a config\n   file or an email without escaping problems. The salt and IV are not secret; they\n   are the recipe, not the ingredients. Inside the encrypted text the two fields are\n   stored with a length in front of each, which is why a name containing an `@` or a\n   `|` cannot be mistaken for the email.\n5. **Decrypting runs this backwards** and checks the tag. If the passphrase is wrong,\n   or one bit of the stored text has changed, the check fails and you get a clear\n   refusal instead of a corrupted name.\n\n### Why these particular choices\n\n- **GCM rather than plain AES-CBC.** With CBC you must also get a message\n  authentication code right, and getting it wrong is a classic and serious mistake.\n  GCM does encryption and integrity in one step and refuses loudly on mismatch.\n- **A key derived from a passphrase rather than a random key in a file.** One less\n  secret to lose, move around and back up. The price is that the passphrase has to be\n  strong, because the 210,000 rounds only slow down a guesser, they do not stop one.\n- **Fresh salt and IV per encryption.** Encrypting the same person twice gives two\n  different tokens. That is on purpose: if identical inputs produced identical\n  tokens, anyone holding the stored data could count how many times each person\n  appears without ever decrypting anything.\n- **210,000 rounds.** This follows the current OWASP recommendation for\n  PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256. It costs about a tenth of a second per call, which nobody\n  notices and an attacker does.\n- **Name and email encrypted together.** They are almost always needed together, and\n  encrypting them as one blob means one tag covers both, so nobody can swap Alice's\n  email onto Bob's name.\n\n### Things it checks before encrypting\n\nThese are not fussiness; each one prevents a specific silent failure:\n\n- **No control characters** in a name or email. A newline or a NUL in PII is nearly\n  always a mistake or an attack, and it wrecks log and file formats.\n- **No half of a two-part character.** Unicode emoji and some scripts are stored as\n  character pairs; if one half goes missing, Java quietly replaces it with a `?` and\n  the name comes back wrong. Better to refuse.\n- **A passphrase must not be empty, only spaces, contain a NUL, or hold half a\n  character.** Verified against the JDK rather than assumed: `PBEKeySpec` really does\n  stop at a NUL and really does turn a stray half-character into `?`, which would\n  otherwise mean two different passphrases open the same record. The test suite\n  proves both, so if a future JDK changes we find out.\n- **Email shape.** A single `@`, something on both sides, no spaces, no `..`, no\n  hyphen at the start or end of a name part, and at least one dot in the host. This\n  is a typo filter, not an RFC 5322 validator; it accepts unusual-but-legal addresses\n  like `first.last+tag@sub.example.co.uk` and international ones, because nothing in\n  the encryption depends on the address being real.\n- **Length limits** (200 characters for a name, 254 for an email) keep records a\n  sane size and stop a runaway input.\n\n## Testing\n\n```\njavac -d out src/*.java test/*.java\njava -cp out PiiCryptoTest\n```\n\nIt prints `OK  194 checks passed` and exits `0`, or lists what failed and exits `1`.\nThe suite takes about 35 seconds, almost all of it spent doing the real key\nstretching the whole point of the design.\n\nIt covers, among other things:\n\n- Names and emails come back **exactly** as they went in, including accents, Chinese\n  characters and emoji, at ordinary sizes and right at the size limits.\n- **A wrong passphrase is refused**, including one that differs by a single last\n  character, and the refusal does not repeat the passphrase back.\n- **Tampering is refused**: a flipped bit in the cipher text, in the tag, in the salt\n  or in the IV; a token cut short; a token with an extra byte glued on; a token\n  claiming a format version this code does not know.\n- **Malformed input is refused**: not Base64, Base64 in the wrong alphabet, spread\n  over two lines, too short to be real, or empty.\n- **Nothing leaks.** The token, and the bytes behind it, do not contain the email,\n  the name, or any recognisable piece of either.\n- **The checks on the decrypt side work**, by hand-building a token around a broken\n  payload. Those exist for tokens written by something other than this program.\n- **The security numbers are the ones this file claims**: 256-bit key, 128-bit tag,\n  16-byte salt, 12-byte IV, at least 200,000 rounds, and a measured lower bound on\n  how long one derivation actually takes. That last one matters: if somebody \"optimises\"\n  the rounds away, the tests fail rather than quietly making stored tokens crackable.\n\nBecause a test suite that passes on everything proves nothing, `.mutation/` breaks the\ncode in 24 specific ways (delete the random bytes, weaken the key, use the lax Base64\ndecoder, remove the control-character check, print the email in `toString`, and so\non) and confirms the suite fails each time:\n\n```\npython3 .mutation/run_mutants.py\n```\n\nIt works on copies, never touches your sources, and takes a few minutes on four\ncores.\n\n## What this program is not\n\nBeing straight about the edges:\n\n- **It protects data at rest, that is all.** It does not cover the name and email\n  while they sit in a database in plain text, appear in a log line, or travel over a\n  network.\n- **Lose the passphrase and the data is gone.** There is no back door, no reset and\n  no second key. That is the correct behaviour for real encryption, and the correct\n  worry for you to have.\n- **Changing a passphrase means re-encrypting.** Decrypt with the old one, encrypt\n  with the new, one record at a time. Nothing here automates that.\n- **One shared passphrase for everything is weak.** Anyone who can encrypt can also\n  decrypt, so it gives you no proof about who wrote a record. Per-person or\n  per-tenant passphrases, or an envelope scheme where a random AES key is itself\n  wrapped with RSA, are the usual next step.\n- **The passphrase is not wiped as thoroughly as it could be.** `PiiApp` zeroes the\n  copies it holds. It cannot reach the copy the Java crypto classes keep internally,\n  which the JDK does not let anyone clear.\n- **A wrong passphrase and a damaged token produce the same message.** Deliberate:\n  telling them apart can hand an attacker free information.\n- **The email check is a typo filter.** Do not use it to decide an address exists.\n- **A demo, not a audited library.** No formal review, no side-channel hardening. For\n  regulated PII, use a vetted encryption library and a key management service.\n\n## Requirements\n\nJava 17 or newer (`java -version` to check). Uses only `javax.crypto` and\n`java.security`, which ship with the JDK. It was written and verified on OpenJDK\n17.0.20.1.\n","file_path":"/data/results/qwen/set_a/sallie/2/basic/README.md"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","usage":{"input_tokens":99280,"output_tokens":3379,"cache_read_input_tokens":0,"total_tokens":102659}}},{"type":"user","uuid":"eafd56b2-c1bc-4843-81e6-22b96ed7e284","session_id":"1a023254-52bc-47d6-a16f-17b5842e79b2","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"tool_result","tool_use_id":"call_a4d73082c9d8414c8f26df47","is_error":false,"content":"Successfully created and wrote to new file: /data/results/qwen/set_a/sallie/2/basic/README.md."}]}},{"type":"assistant","uuid":"18ef8a1b-76a4-42d4-ac97-325c33b41c83","session_id":"1a023254-52bc-47d6-a16f-17b5842e79b2","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"id":"18ef8a1b-76a4-42d4-ac97-325c33b41c83","type":"message","role":"assistant","model":"vllm-general","content":[{"type":"text","text":"I referenced build.sh in the README — I need to create it. If the input is\ninvalid in two places, the guard you did not test can fire first and satisfy the\nmessage needle anyway.\n\n**Why:** Measured 2026-09-12 on an AES-256-GCM PII utility. A mutation battery deleted\nthe `length == 0` branch from a length-prefixed payload parser and the suite stayed\ngreen (1 of 24 survived). The test `a zero-length name is refused` expected the needle\n`corrupt length` and it matched — but it matched because the *second* length field in\nthe hand-built fixture was also garbage, so the email check threw with the same\nmessage class. The name check was never exercised. The check that looked like coverage\nof one guard was really coverage of another. Rebuilding the fixture so only the name\nlength was wrong (`version | len=0 | len=2 | \"ab\"`) killed the mutant with exactly the\nassertion that named the mutated property.\n\n**How to apply:** After writing a malformed-input test, ask which guard actually threw.\nMake the rest of the fixture valid (correct lengths, correct checksum, correct framing)\nso the only possible refusal is the one intended.
